# The Fourth Part

Filter the water clear through a filter paper <note>original: "Maculatur"; coarse or blotting paper used for straining</note>. If there is one pound of it, then take two pounds of **vitriol** <note>metal sulfates, often used to create strong acids</note> and one pound of **saltpeter** <note>potassium nitrate</note>; grind them together. Put the **mercury water** <note>original: "aqua Mercurij"</note> into a receiver <note>a vessel used to collect the results of distillation</note> and drive off the <term>phlegm</term> <note>the watery, inactive part of a distillate</note> along with the <term>spirits</term> <note>the volatile, active essences</note> from the **vitriol** and **saltpeter**, in the same manner as one is accustomed to burn <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>original: "Aquafort"; literally "strong water," a historical name for nitric acid</note>. Afterward, when it is cold, put the water into another flask and distill it over again quite strongly from the sand <note>referring to a sand bath, used to provide steady, high heat</note>, ensuring it is well-sealed with luting <note>a clay or paste used to airtight alchemical joints</note>.

Thus, much red matter remains behind in the flask; this is good to use for old injuries <note>or chronic sores</note>. But for the distilled water—which will be yellowish—draw it over once more in another flask, gently purified <note>original: "rectificirt"</note> from the ashes <note>an ash bath, used for gentler, more controlled heat than sand</note>. Thus a beautiful <term>orange-colored oil</term> <note>original: "Pommerantzenfarbe Oel"</note> remains behind. In this, dissolve as many **corals** <note>original: "Corallen"</note> as you can, for it attacks them quickly and bravely <note>meaning the acid reacts vigorously with the coral</note>.

Pour off the <term>solution</term>. Draw the oil from the **corals** quite strongly, so the **corals** remain beautifully <term>calcined</term> <note>reduced to a friable or powdery state by the action of heat or acid</note> at the bottom <note>original: "in fundo"</note>. ### of Salt-Minerals.

Whatever *remains at the bottom* <note>original: "in fundo"</note>, put that same *matter* into a pot, seal it with lute <note>original: "verlutirs"; using a paste to create an airtight seal</note>, and burn it completely to ashes in a potter’s or brick kiln. Finally, pour the *spirit of wine* off and filter it clear through a *blotting paper* <note>original: "maculatur Papier"; unsized paper used for filtration</note>. You can repeat <note>original: "reiteriren"</note> this several times and clarify the salt until it is as clear and transparent as a crystal. That then is the true salt

### Of the Salt-Minerals.

To use this salt as a medicine, it is done thus: one takes a **dram** <term>quentlein</term> <note>a small unit of weight, roughly 3.6 grams</note> of it in **spirits of wine** <note>original: "Spiritu vini"; high-proof distilled alcohol</note>, or—which is better—**dissolved** <term>resolvirt</term> in **balsam of sulfur** <term>Balsamo sulphuris</term> <note>a preparation of sulfur dissolved in oil, historically used for chest ailments</note>. It drives away **consumption** <term>Schwindsucht</term> <note>a historical term for wasting diseases, most often tuberculosis</note> and heals all afflictions of the lungs, stills shortness of breath, and relieves the side-stitch called **pleurisy** <term>Pleuresis</term> <note>original: "Pleuresis"; an inflammation of the membranes surrounding the lungs</note>. A bath is also made from this salt, along with other appropriate salts, which is a soothing treatment that excellently draws out humors and stills **gout** <term>Podagra</term> <note>original: "Podagra"; a painful form of inflammatory arthritis, usually in the feet</note>, providing relief from that moment on.

Namely thus: One takes 6 **stoups** <term>Stübichen</term> <note>a liquid measure, roughly 3.7 liters per unit</note> of warm water and **dissolves** <term>resolvirt</term> within it 6 pounds of this salt and three pounds of **salt of tartar** <term>Sal tartari</term> <note>original: "Sal tartari"; potassium carbonate, a salt produced by burning the crusty deposits found in wine barrels</note>. Let them come to a boil together; afterward, the hands or legs should be held therein for a period of time, as warm as one can endure it. If this is **repeated** <term>repetirt</term> three or four times, it draws out all the phlegm of the **coagulated matter** <term>coagulirten materia</term> from which the gout takes its **origin** <term>originem</term>, opens the **pores** <term>Poros</term> and sweat-holes, and provides an excellent **resolution** <term>resolutio</term> <note>the process of dissolving or dispersing a hardening or blockage in the body</note> of the hardening and internal stiffness of all the limbs.

But this remedy must be used in a warm bath-chamber, where one can also find cause for a dry sweat-bath; thus one will certainly find what special virtue and power this salt will demonstrate in such treatments.

The **Salt of Urine** <term>Sal Urinae</term> <note>original: "Sal Urinæ"; a salt distilled from urine, considered by early modern chemists to be a potent "animal salt" for healing</note> is also found useful when the **internal governing spirit** <term>Archeus</term> <note>original: "Archeus"; in Paracelsian medicine, this is the "inner alchemist" or vital force located in the stomach that directs health and warding off disease</note> ceases to function and begins to fall into frailty within its salt-essence—often due to a particular influence from the **upper region** <note>a reference to the macrocosm, or astrological influences that were believed to affect the human body's internal state</note>—so that a **restoration** <term>Reductio</term> <note>original: "Reductio"; the alchemical process of returning a substance to its original state or essence</note> and recovery may occur through it. Thus its **first matter** <term>prima materia</term> <note>original: "prima materia"; the fundamental base substance of any living thing</note> can be restored by such a **means** <term>medium</term> and brought back to its former **state** <term>statum</term> for complete **operation** as it was before; for one **salt-spirit** <term>Saltzgeist</term> <note>the volatile or essential essence of a salt</note> can lead and assist another of like kind.

## Talpa, Mole. <note>original: "Talpa, Maulwurff"</note>

Eight **grains** <term>gran</term> <note>a minute unit of weight, roughly 0.06 grams</note> taken with ***diaphoretic gold*** <note>original: "Auro diaphoretico"; a chemical preparation of gold intended to induce sweating to purge toxins</note> / serves against **leprosy** <note>original: "Aussatz"; often used generally for severe or scaling skin diseases</note> / and drives it away. It makes the hair that has fallen out grow back on a person / and when sprinkled into a corrupt **fistula** / it cleanses and heals it.

## Rana, Frog. <note>original: "Rana, Frosch"</note>

Water distilled from frogs or their **spawn** <note>original: "Geleich"</note> / with half a **loth** <term>loth</term> <note>a historical unit of weight, approximately 15-16 grams</note> of salt dissolved in 4 loth [of the water] / and cloths soaked therein / placed upon the sores of the **poisonous pestilence** <note>the plague</note> / draws out all the poison from them / and is an excellent cooling remedy. Likewise, if held in the mouth for a time and constantly refreshed / it draws out all the pus and phlegm from the ailment under the tongue / called **"the frogs"** <note>original: "die Frösche"; likely referring to a *ranula*, a cyst under the tongue that was thought to resemble a frog's throat</note>.

Used in warm wine / for three consecutive days / each time a quarter of a **quentin** <term>quentin</term> <note>a small weight, approximately 4 grams</note> / it takes away all **fever** / that has arisen from a **phlegmatic state** <note>referring to an imbalance of "phlegm," one of the four humors in pre-modern medicine</note>.

### Partridge <note>original: "Perdix" (Latin) and "Rephun" (German)</note>

## Hen <note>original: "Gallina" (Latin) and "Henn" (German)</note>

If half a **lot** <term>loht</term> <note>a historical German unit of weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</note> of this **salt** is taken / in a previously unsalted **chicken broth** / it is a laxative <note>original: "weichung"; literally a "softening" of the bowels</note> / for **constipation** / and serves against **cholera** <note>in this context, likely referring to an excess of yellow bile or "choleric" humors rather than the modern infectious disease</note>. ## Turtledove <note>original: "Turtur" (Latin) and "Turteltaube" (German)</note>

When 3 **grains** of this **salt** / are administered with **Oil of Saturn** <note>original: "Oleo Saturni"; an alchemical preparation of lead, often used in historical medicine to "cool" the body or dampen passions</note> / it makes one **chaste** / and holds back all **unclean desires**. Placed into the **bites of mad people** / who have suffered harm from **rabid dogs** <note>original: "unsinnigen Hunden"; literally "insane" or "senseless" dogs</note> / it draws out the **poison** / and also when given / with **Heath Woundwort** <note>original: "Heidenisch Wundkraut"; likely Goldenrod, *Solidago virgaurea*</note> and **Fleawort water** / which has been boiled / they shall return again to their former health. It is also good / when used for those people / who **bleed too much from the nose**.

### Swallow <note>original: "Hirundo" (Latin) and "Schwalbe" (German)</note>

# Raven <note>original: "Corvus, Rapp/ oder Rabe"</note>

The salt of a raven, when used in a dose of a fourth-part of a <term>quentin <note>a historical German unit of weight, approximately 3.75 grams</note></term> in an <term>oil of the sun <note>original: "Oleo solis"; likely a gold-infused oil or a tincture of solar-aligned herbs used in alchemical medicine</note></term>, keeps a person youthful in constitution, consumes all evil that might occur within the body, and brings a person to a long-lasting age. When raven fat is used for embalmed bodies—alongside the salt made from peacock flesh—they never decay. Taken in <term>spirit of wine <note>original: "spiritu vini"; distilled alcohol or brandy</note></term>, it serves against the <term>falling sickness <note>original: "hinfallenden Sucht"; a historical term for epilepsy</note></term> and is useful against <term>the stroke <note>original: "dem Schlage"; apoplexy</note></term>.

Whosoever has <term>consumption <note>original: "Schwindsucht"; a wasting disease, usually tuberculosis</note></term>, let him take the aforementioned weight of this salt in a distilled oil made from <term>human fat <note>original: "Menschenfett"; human-derived fat was a documented ingredient in the early modern pharmacopeia, often sourced from executioners</note></term>—it drives the disease away—or in the oil distilled from <term>dog fat <note>original: "Hundeschmaltz"</note></term>. Used in <term>cinnamon water <note>original: "aqua Cinamomi"</note></term>, it strengthens the brain and creates a good memory.

### Heron <note>original: "Ardea, Reyger"</note>

Whosoever is burdened with <term>dropsy <note>original: "Wassersucht"; a historical term for edema or the swelling of soft tissues due to excess water</note></term>, let him take the water distilled from <term>toadflax <note>original: "Heidenflachs"; Linaria vulgaris, a plant traditionally used as a diuretic</note></term> and dissolve one <term>quentin <note>approximately 3.75 grams</note></term> of this salt of the heron within it. He should use it three times every day—morning, noon, and evening—and ensure he sweats well after each dose. He will be released from the condition in a very few days; for it not only drives out the sweat, but also the urine powerfully, and carries away all wateriness and excess <term>phlegm <note>original: "Phlegma"; in the theory of the four humors, an excess of phlegm was thought to cause sluggishness and swelling</note></term> of the body through the <term>urine <note>original: "Urinam"</note></term>.

This salt, mixed into its own heron-fat and made into a small ointment, restores paralyzed limbs when they are smeared and well rubbed with it. It also softens hard, stiffened <term>veins <note>original: "Adern"; in this context, it likely refers to tendons or sinews that have become contracted or "too short"</note></term>. Likewise, for any person whose limb is <term>wasting away <note>original: "schwindet"; referring to muscle atrophy or consumption of the limb</note></term>, let them smear this <term>prepared <note>original: "præparirte"</note></term> ointment twice every day near a heat source; it restores such a limb to its proper state.

# The Fourth Part

## Hoopoe <note>original: "Upupa, Widhopff"</note>

Whoever feels a great <term>headache <note>original: "Hauptwehe"</note></term> arising from <term>supernatural heat <note>original: "vbernatuͤrlicher Hitze"; in historical medicine, this refers to an unnatural fever or inflammatory state caused by an imbalance of the humors</note></term>, let him dissolve this salt in <term>rose water <note>original: "Rosenwasser"; water distilled from rose petals, valued for its cooling properties</note></term>. He should then soak a small linen cloth in this mixture and apply it to the forehead several times; it draws out all heat, soothes and stills the pains, and also grants clear vision when the eyes are brushed with this water. Taken internally with <term>spirits <note>original: "Aqua vitæ"; literally "water of life," usually referring to distilled wine or brandy</note></term>, in a dose of 6 or 8 <term>grains <note>original: "gran"; a small unit of weight approximately equal to 0.06 grams</note></term> every morning and evening, it consumes the <term>head-catarrhs <note>original: "Heuptfluͤß"; the "flowing" of excess humors from the head, thought to cause various illnesses</note></term> and produces a good memory.

## Starling <note>original: "Sturnus, Stahr"</note>

When one uses starling salt—a fourth part of a <term>quentin <note>original: "Quentlein"; roughly 0.9 grams in this proportion</note></term> dissolved in the juice of <term>bitter oranges <note>original: "gelben Pomerantzen"</note></term> or an <term>extract of bitter orange peels <note>original: "extractio der Pomerantzenschalen"</note></term>—it provides an excellent cooling for a heart overburdened with heat. Rubbed together with <term>white lead <note>original: "Bleyweis"; basic lead carbonate, historically used in ointments despite its toxicity</note></term>, <term>litharge <note>original: "Glet"; lead monoxide</note></term>, and <term>olive oil <note>original: "Baumoͤl"; literally "tree oil"</note></term> into an <term>ointment <note>original: "Vnguento"</note></term>, it drives away <term>warts <note>original: "Feigblattern"; specifically "fig-warts," often referring to skin growths or hemorrhoids</note></term> and blemishes of the face, as well as all <term>rashes <note>original: "Masern"; while modern "measles," here it likely refers to any spotted skin eruption</note></term> of the body.

## Herring <note>original: "Halec"</note>.

The salt of **herring**, taken daily in the amount of one <term>scruple <note>original: "scrupel"; an apothecary weight equal to 20 grains or about 1.3 grams</note></term> in **fennel water**, creates good vision and consumes the "darkness" <note>likely referring to cataracts or general clouding of vision</note> of the eyes. This salt, made into an ointment with an equal amount of **crushed sulfur** and mixed with **lard**, drives away all mange <note>original: "Räudigkeit"; a scaly or itchy skin condition</note> of the skin when applied, and makes the skin gentle, soft, and tender.

## Land Snails <note>original: "Cocleæ terrestres"</note>.

**Snail salt** creates a good memory and serves to treat **bladder and kidney stones** <note>original: "Lendenstein"; literally "loin-stone"</note>. When the snail is burned to ash along with its little house <note>the shell</note> and made into salt, it brings about a peaceful mind. It is also good for all hardening of the nerves and resolves <term>gout <note>original: "Podagra"; a painful inflammatory arthritis, historically often associated with the feet</note></term>, especially when its <term>spirit <note>original: "Spiritus"; in early chemistry, a volatile liquid obtained through distillation</note></term> is "driven over" <note>distilled</note>. This is to be understood of all salts whenever gout and similar ailments are mentioned.

It also creates a good voice, dispels hoarseness, and produces good blood.

# ON PLANTS <note>original: "DE VEGETABILIBVS"; referring to the plant kingdom</note>

## How the salts <note>original: "Salia"</note> are to be extracted from all herbs and plants.

Take whichever herb you wish / burn it to ashes / make a lye from it / with warm water / coagulate the lye / so the salt remains at the **bottom** <note>original: "in fundo"</note>, dissolve that in **spirit of wine** <note>original: "Spiritu vini"; a high-proof alcohol used as a solvent</note>, the **dregs** <note>original: "feces"; the solid remains or sediment</note>, which will settle / cast away / draw off the **spirit of wine** through **distillation** <note>original: "per diſtillatiōnē"</note>, and dissolve it as often [as needed] / until the salt is beautifully pure and clear / and no more **dregs** settle / then it is ready. When one correctly handles the purification with the **spirit of wine** <note>original: "S. v."</note> / all **salts** of the herbs can be brought to such a state / so clear / beautiful and pure / that they form into transparent **crystals** / like a clear / pure / refined **saltpeter** <note>potassium nitrate</note>.

In this way, the salt is also made from **roots** / and from **wood** / Likewise from **aromatics** <note>original: "Aromatibus"</note> or **spices**.

<meta>catchword: Use (original: "Vſus")</meta>

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process for extracting and purifying salts from plants, roots, wood, and spices using incineration, lye creation, and distillation with alcohol. The goal is to produce high-purity, transparent crystals comparable to refined saltpeter.</summary>
<keywords>alkali salts, plant extraction, distillation, spirit of wine, lye, crystallization, saltpeter, aromatics</keywords>

<page-num>302</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Part: USES.</header>

### Absinthium, Wormwood.

Wormwood salt promotes the flow of urine / drives away colic / settles the winds <note>internal gas or flatulence</note> in the body / promotes sweating / is good for all long-lasting fevers / consumes the evil <note>impurities or acidity</note> in the stomach / brings an appetite for eating / cleanses the blood / It is good, used at the time of the Pestilence <note>The Plague</note> / taken every morning, half a quentin <note>original: "quent"; a historical unit of weight, approximately 3.7 grams</note> in aged wine <note>original: "Firnemwein"; a matured, high-quality wine</note> / and then sweating upon it. Externally, make with this salt / white lead <note>a white pigment and medicinal base, now known to be toxic</note> and a little olive oil <note>original: "Baumöl"; literally "tree oil," usually referring to olive oil</note> / an ointment / and apply it with cotton / or scraped lint from linen cloth / onto fig-warts <note>original: "Feigwarzen"; a historical term for various skin growths, including hemorrhoids or condyloma</note> and similar sores / It heals such things fundamentally.

### Borago, Borage.

Makes good blood / strengthens the heart / for the three-day <note>tertian</note> and quartan fever / drives out through sweating <note>original: "per sudorem"</note> everything evil / just like the salt of wormwood <note>original: "Sal abſynthij"</note>, Also its weight <note>dosage</note> is like the wormwood / in aged wine.

### Chamomile.

Promotes the menstrual flow and the urine / expels the bladder stone / and rectifies all ailments of the liver / Its weight <note>dosage</note> is as stated above, taken in aged wine <note>original: "firnem Wein"; a matured, high-quality wine</note>.

### Angelica.

Serves against the Plague / a half-quentin <note>original: "quente"; a historical unit of weight, approximately 3.7 grams</note> taken with rue vinegar / and pressed juniper juice. Taken with anise oil / it renovates the blood / likewise the brain <note>original: "Cerebrum"</note> / and drives away all fluxes <note>excessive fluid or congestion</note> of the head.

### Mugwort.

Two drachms <note>original: "Drachmas"; a historical unit of weight</note> taken with five or six drops of myrrh oil / or oil of juniper <note>original: "Oleo Juniperi"</note> in wine / expels and breaks the stone / promotes the urine / and opens the closed womb <note>original: "verschlossene Mutter"; refers to uterine obstructions or difficulties with menstruation</note>.

## Betony (*Betonica*).

One *quentin* <note>original: "quentē"; a historical unit of weight, approximately 3.7 grams</note> taken in wine / expels the poison / should a person have received any. It also serves for kidney ailments / and applied externally / sprinkled upon the bites of poisonous animals.

## St. John’s Wort (*Hypericum*).

Promotes the urine and the menstrual flow <note>original: "Menstruum"</note> / taken with lemon balm water / 6 or 8 grains <note>approx. 0.4 to 0.5 grams</note>. Taken with boiled speedwell <note>original: "Ehrenpreiß"; a herb of the genus *Veronica*</note> water / it is a preservative <note>original: "Præservatiff"; a preventative or protective remedy</note> for all wounds / and when the oil of St. John’s Wort <note>original: "Oleum Hypericonis"</note> is applied externally / it heals them thoroughly.

## Burnet Saxifrage (*Pimpinella*).

Its salt serves for old injuries <note>original: "alten schäden"; chronic wounds or sores</note> / and heals miraculously / serves also for the pestilence <note>the plague</note> / stills the women's time <note>original: "Weiber zeit"</note> / the menstrual flow and the "red flux" <note>original: "rote Ruhr"; dysentery</note> / purifies the blood / lungs / liver and kidneys / and is used against the "rising of the mother" <note>original: "auffsteigen der Mutter"; a historical medical term for hysteria or uterine displacement</note>.

### Guaiacum, or "Frenchman’s Wood" (*Guaiacum*).

When the liquid water and the oil have been driven off from the wood through distillation in a dry bath <note>original: "Balneo sicco"; a method of heating using sand or ash rather than water to reach higher temperatures</note> / then the wood is burned to ashes / and its salt is extracted / just as from other things. This salt serves for the "French disease" <note>syphilis</note> / or the Gallic way <term>modum Gallicum</term> / one quent <note>approx. 3.7 grams</note> taken / with six grains <note>approx. 0.38 grams</note> of its distilled liquid in wine / with fumitory water <note>original: "Erdrauchwasser"; a medicinal herb used for skin and liver ailments</note> / and three or four grains used with Turpeth Mineral <term>turpero Minerali</term> <note>a basic mercuric sulfate used historically as a powerful purgative and treatment for syphilis</note> / it powerfully drives out sweat and urine. Dissolved in a little of its oil / and spread upon all stinking sores / which are open, rotten, and caused by the French disease / it cleanses and heals them.

## Persicaria, Smartweed <note>also known as Water-pepper</note>.

The salt of Persicaria is a magnificent <term>arcanum</term> <note>a "secret" or powerful alchemical remedy</note> for old, rotting, and foul-smelling wounds. It is also used for cancer, fistulas, "the wolf" <note>a historical term for eating sores or lupus</note>, shingles, and tetter <note>original: *Zittrachten*, a general term for skin eruptions like eczema or ringworm</note> when sprinkled externally upon them. However, for internal use, one should also use a good "wound-drink" made in this manner:

Take one and a half <term>loht</term> <note>a historical German weight, roughly 15 grams or half an ounce</note> of rhubarb / two loht of sweet flag / five handfuls of soapwort / one loht each of myrrh, mastic, and frankincense / and one loht of orris root <note>original: *Violwurtzel*</note>. Let these be well-seethed or boiled in common water / then strained clean / and dissolve four loht of this salt <note>the salt of Persicaria</note> into it. When used, it demonstrates its virtue and power to great wonder in all injuries.

## Salvia, Sage.

It draws the blood out of wounds / and cleanses evil ulcers / when applied externally. One <term>quentin</term> <note>a small weight, about 3.7 grams</note> taken with wine in the morning / creates good blood internally / and makes the heart joyful.

<header>The Fourth Part</header>

## Persicaria, Water Pepper.

The salt of Persicaria <note>original: *Wasserpfeffer*, a plant of the knotweed family known for its pungent taste</note> is a magnificent <term>Arcanum</term> <note>a secret or highly potent alchemical remedy</note> for old, putrid, and foul-smelling sores. It is also excellent for cancer, fistulas, the "wolf" <note>original: *Wolff*, a historical term for lupus or corrosive ulcers</note>, skin eruptions <note>original: *Sireyflechten*</note>, and tetters <note>original: *Zittrachten*, various scaly skin diseases</note> when sprinkled therein externally. However, for internal use, one should also use a good "wound-drink" <note>a healing tonic or decoction</note> made in this way:

Take one and a half <term>lot</term> <note>a historical unit of weight, approximately 14.6 grams or half an ounce</note> of Rhubarb, 2 lots of Calamus <note>Sweet Flag</note>, five handfuls of Soapwort, one lot each of Myrrh, Mastic, and Frankincense, and one lot of Orris root <note>original: *Violwurtzel*</note>. Let these be well-seethed or well-boiled in common water, then cleanly strained. Afterward, dissolve 4 lots of this salt within it. This, when used, demonstrates its virtue and power to great wonder in all manner of injuries.

## Salvia, Sage.

Stops the bleeding from wounds and cleanses malignant ulcers when applied to them externally. When one <term>quentin</term> <note>a small unit of weight, about 3.7 grams</note> is used with wine in the morning, it creates good blood internally and makes the heart joyful.

## Roses <note>original: *Rosa, Rosen*</note>.

A quarter of a <term>quint</term> <note>a quint was a historical unit of weight, roughly 3.7 to 4 grams</note> taken in a <term>lot</term> <note>approximately 15 grams</note> of cinnamon water drives away heart palpitations <note>original: *Hertzittern*; literally "heart-trembling"</note>, strengthens the heart and the brain, makes good blood, and strengthens the head. It drives away <term>melancholy</term> and superfluous <term>phlegm</term> <note>Two of the "four humors" believed to regulate health; an excess of phlegm was thought to cause sluggishness or respiratory issues</note>, from which runny eyes <note>original: *Augenflüsse*; referring to rheum or discharge</note> arise; it drives away fever and opens obstructions of the liver.

Dissolved in rose vinegar, it draws out the heat of the <term>pestilence</term> <note>The plague</note> and all other evil boils and inflamed pox <note>original: *hitzigen Blattern*; likely referring to smallpox or similar pustules</note>. When applied as a compress <note>original: *vbergeschlagen*</note>, it also draws to itself all the heat of the head and the soles of the feet.

## *Rosa transmarina* <note>Latin: "Overseas Rose"</note>, true Autumn Roses.

Also, when drunk in this manner, it stills all internal heat of the body.

<header>of Salt-Minerals <note>In early modern pharmacy, "salt-minerals" or "plant salts" were created by burning plant matter to ashes, leaching them with water, and evaporating the liquid to leave behind concentrated mineral salts believed to hold the plant's essential healing power.</note>.</header>

### Gooseberry <note>original: *Vva Crispa, Stachdorn*; literally "thorny thorn," referring here to the prickly gooseberry bush</note>.

Half a <term>quint</term> <note>a historical unit of weight, roughly 3.7 to 4 grams</note> dissolved in its own [distilled] water and given to those who have great tearing <note>rheumatic or pulling pains</note>, stinging, and aches in the arms and legs; and the herb of the gooseberry, moistened with wine vinegar, well-crushed in a mortar, and applied warm [as a compress], drives away such pains, stinging, and aches. It also resists heart palpitations <note>original: *Hertzzittern*; literally "heart-trembling"</note> and stitches in the side, as well as the great throbbing and shooting pains in the head.

## CONCERNING AROMATICS <note>original Latin: *DE AROMATIBVS*</note>

### Concerning the Spices.

When the <term>essential oils</term> <note>original: *Oliteten*</note> have been drawn off from the spices, the <term>residue</term> <note>original Latin: *residuum*; the material remaining after distillation</note> is placed in a pot and burnt to ash; then its <term>salts</term> <note>original Latin: *Salia*</note> are extracted <term>with spirit of wine</term> <note>original Latin: *cum Spiritu vini*; high-proof alcohol or brandy used as a solvent</note>, just as was done with the herbs.

### Ginger <note>original: *Zinziber, Ingber*</note>

## Wine Vinegar Lees <note>original: *Fex aceti*; the sediment or dregs of vinegar</note>

When the wine vinegar has been distilled / the turbidity and oiliness <note>original: *Olitet*; the viscous part of the distillate</note> are driven over particularly thereafter / Finally, whatever dregs <note>original: *Fæces*</note> remain behind / burn those in a pot to ashes / and extract its salt from them / as has been done with the others. # Tartar, Wine-stone <note>original: *Tartarus, Weinstein*; the crystalline crust that forms in wine barrels, chemically known as potassium bitartrate</note>.

From Tartar, many kinds of medicines are prepared / both internal and external / but now I will write only of the most noble / and provide a report on those / which have shown themselves as such in experience / and have made their proof evident. Firstly, however, a liquid <term>Liquor</term> <note>refers to "Oil of Tartar per deliquium," a concentrated solution of potassium carbonate</note> is made from it, namely by putting the Tartar into a pot / and calcining it <note>roasting at high heat to reduce it to ash</note> in a blast furnace <term>Windofen</term> / and burning it until it is white / thereafter it is rubbed small, laid upon a stone in a cellar / and put into a pointed woolen bag / and hung up / so that the liquid flows out / catch this in a glass. This is now the common method / which is known to almost everyone. This liquid is good for all injuries / that have not yet taken over too much; It also effects a healing therein / cleanses scabby skin and the itch <note>original: *Kräße*; likely referring to scabies or similar skin infections</note> / makes the skin smooth and clean / and drives away the tetter <note>original: *Zittracht*; an archaic term for various skin eruptions</note> and lichen on the hands / when smeared upon them.

TAKE Saltpeter and white Arsenic <note>original: *Arsenicū*; arsenic trioxide</note> / an equal amount of each / rub them well together until small / put it into a melting crucible / and set the crucible in a blast furnace <term>Windofen</term> / give it good fire / so that the material flows well / entirely glowing for one hour / Afterward pour the material out / however much it weighs / rub an equal amount of Salt of Tartar <term>Sal Tartari</term> into it / place it upon a stone / and in a small woolen bag / as mentioned before / in a cellar / so it flows into an Oil <note>This refers to deliquescence, where a substance absorbs atmospheric moisture until it dissolves into a liquid.</note>. When you have this **spirit of tartar** <note>original: *Spiritum tartari*; a volatile liquid distilled from potassium bitartrate</note>, take one part of it, two parts of **spirit of wine** <note>original: *Spiritum vini*; high-proof alcohol or ethanol</note>, an eighth part of **oil of sulfur** <note>original: *Oleum sulphuris*</note>, and a sixteenth part of the red **oil of vitriol** <note>original: *Oleum victrioli*; concentrated sulfuric acid</note>. Ten or twelve grains taken at one time drives away bladder and kidney stones <note>original: *Lendenstein*; literally "loin-stones"</note> and breaks them **miraculously**; it **radically** dissolves **gout** <note>original: *Podagra*; specifically gout of the foot</note>, heals pulmonary disease <note>original: *Lungensucht*</note>, and drives away wasting disease <note>original: *Schwindsucht*; typically referring to tuberculosis</note> and the cough. it provides the stomach with good digestion, opens the **pores** to sweating, and allows all the nerves to be permeated. It brings good blood and an appetite for eating and drinking. And in **summary**, it is a **medicine** and remedy to be used both internally and externally, which performs and proves its worth to great wonder.

### Water of Tartar. <note>original: *Aqua Tartari*</note>

Take raw **tartar** <note>original: *Weinstein*; the potassium bitartrate crusts found in wine barrels</note> and **saltpeter**, equal parts rubbed together, and take an earthenware **flask** <note>original: *Kolben*; likely a retort in this context</note> that has a hollow tube at the back, or a <term>breathing hole</term> <note>original: *spiraculum*; an opening in an alchemical vessel to manage pressure or add materials</note>; place it in a <term>distillation furnace</term> <note>original: *distillir-ofen*</note> in the open fire, and place a glass <term>still-head</term> <note>original: *Helm*; the cap of an alembic that directs vapors into the cooling neck</note> upon it; also <term>seal</term> <note>original: *verlutir*; to apply "lute," a clay-like paste used to make joints airtight</note> a <term>receiver vessel</term> <note>original: *Vorlage*; the flask that collects the final liquid</note> to it. 

When the flask has become glowing hot, throw several glowing coals into the flask through the hollow tube. When this is done, take a spoonful of the rubbed material and pour it into the flask onto the coals; the material will then ignite and give off a great vapor. Plug the tube well with a wet rag. When the vapor has settled <note>condensed into liquid</note>, add more material into the flask and plug it again; repeat this as often and as long as necessary until you have distilled all the material. In this way, you obtain a beautiful, transparent, golden-yellow water, without any <term>corrosiveness</term> <note>original: *Corrosiff*; referring to a lack of burning or caustic acidity</note>.

This water is safe and good to use both internally and externally. Internally, one

### Balsam of Tartar. <note>original: *Balsamus Tartari*</note>

Take white **calcined tartar** <note>original: *weis calcinirten Weinstein*; tartar that has been heated until it turns into a white, alkaline ash (potassium carbonate)</note>; rub it small, put it into a glass **flask** <note>original: *Kolbenglaß*</note>, and pour a strong **aqua fortis** <note>original: *Aquafort*; literally "strong water," a potent mineral acid</note> upon it, distilled from one part **vitriol** <note>original: *Victriol*; metallic sulfates</note>, one part **saltpeter**, half a part **alum**, and a fourth part **verdigris** <note>original: *Grünspan*; a green pigment/chemical formed on copper</note>. 

Distill the water off from it; thus the **phlegm** <note>original: *Phlegma*; the watery, inactive portion of a distillate</note> passes over, but the **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritus*; the volatile, active acidic essences</note> remain behind in the **tartar** <note>original: *Tartaro*</note>. Pour new water upon it and distill it off again; do this several times until the tartar can no longer absorb any more spirits into itself, but rather lets them pass off again. 

Afterwards, take the prepared tartar, rub it small again upon a marble stone, and place it in a cellar to **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*; the process of deliquescence, where a salt absorbs moisture from the damp cellar air to turn into a liquid</note>, as mentioned before; thus it flows into a **liquor** <note>original: *Liquorem*</note>. Of this clear liquor, take one part; fresh— <note>The text ends here mid-sentence, likely continuing with the addition of a fresh ingredient on the following page.</note>

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;And take great offense at it.
Everything that the Trinity
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Created from the beginning at that same time,
Is all good for the pious,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;But does harm to the ungodly. <note>A paraphrase of Sirach 39:27: "All these things are good for the godly, but for sinners they are turned into evils."</note>
For man requires in this world,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;As presented here for his life:
Water, fire, iron, flour, and salt,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Honey, milk, wine, also oil and fat, <note>original: *schmalz*; while this literally means lard or melted fat, in this context it refers to essential animal fats or sustenance.</note>
Also clothes to cover himself;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;All this God has prepared.
So thank Him for it and honor Him;
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;He will not grant them to the wicked.
When I began and wrote this book,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;I thought of the works of the Lord:
That all such things exist according to order,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Rightly ordained by God,
And what He has undertaken for Himself,
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;All comes to the benefit of mankind.
Therefore, praise at all times
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The name of the Lord for eternity,
And praise Him at every hour
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;For such kindness, with heart and mouth,
So that we may all together
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Travel to Him in the eternal hall of joy. <note>original: *Freudensaal*; a common Baroque poetic term for Heaven.</note> Amen.

->J. T. H.<-

-> <image-desc>A small printer's ornament consisting of an asterisk within parentheses with decorative flourishes on either side.</image-desc> <-

> He who teaches how life is to be drawn from rigid metals, <note>original: Vita sit è rigidis qui deducenda metallis... docet</note>
> And what the power and method of the hidden essence may be,
> Great in art, but greater still in his richly endowed genius,
> Greatest of all in his refined heart is Basil. <note>Referring to Basil Valentine, the legendary alchemist and monk.</note>
> Basil, worthy of never being consumed by any age,
> But through his life to equal the highest Gods.
> Recently, reckless Envy, allied with the decay of time, <note>original: Situ; referring to the mold or neglect that hides ancient manuscripts.</note>
> Was weaving snares for the brother:
> But THÖLDE, recognizing the dreadful plots, <note>Johann Thölde was the chemist and editor who first published Basil Valentine's works in the early 1600s.</note>
> Consigns the dark fury, along with the brother, to the Stygian Furies. <note>The Stygian Furies are the vengeful deities of the underworld in Greek mythology; the poet suggests Thölde rescued the work from being forgotten or attacked by critics.</note>
> Long live Basil; long live Thölde also; the former
> Flourishing by the help of the latter; and by the gift of God.

-> Willichius Westhovius <-
-> from Westhofen, a Holsteiner, <term>Poet Laureate</term> <- <note>A Poet Laureate (P. L.) was a poet officially honored for their skill, often by an emperor or university.</note>

<image-desc>A woodcut tailpiece featuring three stylized grotesque heads or snail-like creatures joined in a circular, rotating formation.</image-desc>

<summary>This page concludes the alphabetical index of a chemical treatise and features a laudatory poem by Willichius Westhovius. The poem praises Johann Thölde for preserving the alchemical wisdom of Basil Valentine from the "envy and decay" of time.</summary>
